MPT Answer Keys and Explanations

Numbered to match the practice tests in your printed study guide.

Practice Test 1 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is C (700,000, since the 7 sits in the hundred thousands place): counting from the right, the digits are ones, tens, hundreds, thousands, ten thousands, then hundred thousands, placing the 7 there. Option B misreads the place one column to the right. Option A shifts the digit two columns off from its true position. Option D confuses face value with the digit's actual place value in the number.
  2. The correct answer is B (300 + 5 + 0.4 + 0.007): the digits after the decimal point are tenths, hundredths, thousandths, so 4 is 0.4 and 7 is 0.007. Option A misplaces the 4 and 5 into the wrong columns entirely. Option C fails to break the decimal portion into separate place values. Option D correctly places 0.4 but drops the thousandths place for the final 7.
  3. The correct answer is B (0.5 is larger because 0.50 has a greater tenths digit than 0.45): writing 0.5 as 0.50 shows 5 tenths versus 4 tenths and 5 hundredths, so 0.5 wins on the very first decimal place. Option A wrongly assumes more digits means a larger value. Option C is false since the two numbers round differently at the hundredths level. Option D reverses the actual place value hierarchy, since tenths are worth more than hundredths.
  4. The correct answer is C (Point P at 0.3, because 0.3 is less than 0.62 on the line): comparing tenths shows 3 tenths is less than 6 tenths, so P sits closer to zero. Option A uses digit count as a reason instead of actual value, which is not valid logic. Option B compares the digits as whole numbers instead of as decimals. Option D ignores the clear numeric gap shown between the two plotted points.
  5. The correct answer is D (68,000, since the hundreds digit 4 rounds down): the digit right after the thousands place is 4, which is below 5, so the thousands digit stays the same and the rest become zeros. Option B incorrectly rounds up despite the 4 signaling rounding down. Option C only partially rounds and leaves an invalid remainder in the hundreds place. Option A rounds to the wrong place value entirely, ten thousands instead of thousands.
  6. The correct answer is B (604,012, because the hundreds and tens places need placeholder zeros): twelve only fills the tens and ones places, so a zero placeholder is required in the hundreds place. Option A rearranges digits instead of respecting place value order. Option C adds an extra unnecessary digit that was never part of the number. Option D drops a needed digit from the hundred thousands place entirely.
  7. The correct answer is C (3.08 is greater because 8 hundredths exceeds 8 thousandths): rewriting 3.08 as 3.080 shows 80 thousandths versus only 8 thousandths in 3.008, so 3.08 is clearly larger. Option A wrongly assumes matching digits means equal value regardless of placement. Option B assumes more digits automatically means a bigger number, which is false. Option D confuses smaller place value units with a larger overall quantity.
  8. The correct answer is A (9, because counting from the right gives ones, tens, hundreds, thousands, then ten thousands): the digit 9 lands in the fifth position from the right, which is the ten thousands place. Option B picks a digit based on visual position rather than counted place value. Option C names the thousands-place digit instead of the ten-thousands-place digit. Option D miscounts the position by one column.
  9. The correct answer is B (0.07, 0.7, 0.77): 0.07 is seven hundredths, 0.7 is seventy hundredths, and 0.77 is seventy seven hundredths, giving that exact increasing order. Option A places the largest tenths value in the wrong early position. Option C lists the numbers from greatest to least instead of least to greatest. Option D scrambles the middle and last values out of correct numeric order.
  10. The correct answer is B (Correct, since each addend lines up with its matching place value): 5,000 gives the thousands digit, 300 the hundreds, 20 the tens, and 6 the ones, matching 5,326 exactly. Option A swaps the hundreds and tens digits incorrectly. Option C reverses the tens and ones digits from the true value. Option D wrongly claims a missing digit when all four place values are already accounted for.
  11. The correct answer is B (It does not change the value, since the added zero holds an empty hundredths place): 2.5 already equals five tenths and zero hundredths, so writing the zero explicitly just makes that existing value visible. Option A wrongly assumes any extra digit changes magnitude. Option C describes multiplying by ten, which is a completely different operation. Option D adds an unnecessary and incorrect restriction to a rule that applies to any decimal.
  12. The correct answer is D (40, because the point sits left of the midpoint between 40 and 50): the plotted value of 43 is closer to 40 than to 50, so it rounds down. Option B misreads which side of the midpoint the point actually falls on. Option C names the midpoint itself rather than the rounded result. Option A states a false general rule, since rounding depends on distance, not a fixed preference for larger numbers.
  13. The correct answer is B (9 hundredths, or 0.09): counting after the decimal point gives tenths, hundredths, thousandths, and the 9 sits in the second position, the hundredths place. Option A misplaces the digit one column to the left. Option C misplaces the digit one column to the right. Option D confuses a decimal digit with a whole number place entirely.
  14. The correct answer is C (True, since one million equals 1 followed by six zeros): the place values from ones up to millions are ones, tens, hundreds, thousands, ten thousands, hundred thousands, then millions, which is six zero-filled places. Option B undercounts the zeros by one. Option A overcounts the zeros by one. Option D adds an irrelevant condition about comma formatting that has no bearing on the digit count.
  15. The correct answer is C (They are equal, since trailing zeros after the last nonzero digit add no value): 0.6 and 0.600 both represent six tenths and zero in every place beyond that. Option A wrongly assumes extra decimal digits automatically raise value. Option B uses symbol count as an irrelevant justification. Option D reverses the true place value hierarchy, since thousandths are smaller, not larger, than tenths.
  16. The correct answer is B (Correct, since the thousandths digit 2 rounds the hundredths digit down): the digit after the hundredths place is 2, which is below 5, so the hundredths digit stays as 5. Option A wrongly rounds the hundredths digit up despite the 2 signaling otherwise. Option C rounds to the wrong place value, tenths instead of hundredths. Option D changes the hundredths digit to zero for no valid mathematical reason.
  17. The correct answer is B (900,005): nine hundred thousand fills the hundred thousands place, and five fills only the ones place, requiring zero placeholders in every column between them. Option A misplaces the five into the hundreds column instead of the ones column. Option C drops a needed digit from the hundred thousands place. Option D adds an extra unneeded digit, shifting the number into the millions.
  18. The correct answer is A (0.9 is nine tenths, which is 0.1 less than one whole unit): the digit 9 sits in the tenths place, so its value is 9/10, not 9 whole units. Option B misreads the tenths digit as if it represented whole ones. Option C uses the wrong denominator, confusing tenths with hundredths. Option D compares the digits themselves instead of their actual place values.
  19. The correct answer is A (Correct, since comparing hundreds and tens digits confirms this order): all three share the same thousands digits, so comparing the hundreds place shows 5 is greatest, then 4, then 0. Option B places a smaller hundreds digit ahead of a larger one. Option C puts the smallest value first, which contradicts descending order. Option D is false since the three numbers have clearly different digit values.
  20. The correct answer is C (Yes, because 0.25 equals 25 hundredths, the same value as one quarter): one quarter of a dollar is 25 cents, and 25 hundredths written as a decimal is exactly 0.25. Option A incorrectly claims a value difference that does not exist. Option B wrongly denies that fractions and decimals can represent identical quantities. Option D adds an unnecessary rounding condition to an equality that is already exact.
  21. The correct answer is B (Three nonzero digits): the digits 3, 4, and 7 are nonzero, while the remaining positions hold zero placeholders. Option A undercounts by missing one of the three nonzero digits. Option C overcounts by including a zero as though it were meaningful. Option D overcounts even further, treating multiple placeholders as nonzero digits.
  22. The correct answer is C (Point M at negative 6, because it lies farther left on the line): on a number line, values decrease moving left, so negative 6 is less than negative 2. Option B gives a true observation about proximity but not a valid reason for smaller value. Option A confuses digit size with actual signed value, a common integer misconception. Option D ignores the clearly different positions of the two plotted points.
  23. Option D only rounds the wrong place value and ignores the requested precision.
  24. Option A misreads the decimal at the wrong, larger place value. Option B is still one place value too large for this number. Option D overstates the value by a factor of ten.
  25. Option A picks the smallest place value based only on its position in the written number. Option C selects a middle-sized place value rather than the largest one. Option D is false, since each place value contributes a different amount per single digit increase.
  26. The correct answer is B (Negative 5, since the smaller absolute value is subtracted from the larger and the sign of the larger value is kept): 8 minus 3 is 5, and since 8 has the larger absolute value and is negative, the answer stays negative. Option A wrongly adds the absolute values instead of finding their difference. Option C drops the sign entirely, losing essential information about direction. Option D assumes addition always yields a positive value, which is false for signed numbers.
  27. The correct answer is D (Negative 3, since subtracting a negative becomes adding its opposite): negative 7 minus negative 4 becomes negative 7 plus 4, which equals negative 3. Option B wrongly treats subtracting a negative like adding two negatives together. Option C misapplies a multiplication rule about two negatives to an addition or subtraction situation. Option A ignores the signs entirely rather than tracking them through the operation.
  28. The correct answer is B (14 degrees, since the distance from 5 to negative 9 spans both sides of zero): the drop from 5 to 0 is 5 degrees, and from 0 to negative 9 is another 9 degrees, totaling 14. Option A subtracts the digits without accounting for crossing zero correctly. Option C only counts the distance from zero to the ending temperature. Option D only counts the distance from zero to the starting temperature.
  29. Option A wrongly keeps a negative sign despite both factors being negative. Option B confuses multiplication with addition of the two values. Option D applies an invented halving rule that has no basis in integer multiplication.
  30. The correct answer is D (3, since moving right from negative 4 by 7 units lands past zero): starting at negative 4 and moving 7 units right passes through zero and lands on positive 3. Option B wrongly adds the magnitudes as if the direction did not matter. Option C undercounts the total distance traveled along the line. Option A removes the starting sign instead of tracking the actual movement.
  31. Option A swaps division for subtraction entirely, producing an unrelated result.
  32. The correct answer is B (The student is incorrect, the absolute value of negative 15 is 15): absolute value measures distance from zero, which is always non negative, so negative 15 becomes 15.   237. The correct answer is D (Complementary angles): complementary angles are defined as two angles whose measures sum to exactly 90 degrees, often forming a right angle when combined. Reflex angles are a single angle type measuring between 180 and 360 degrees, not a pair relationship at all.
  238. The correct answer is A (Exactly one pair of parallel sides): a trapezoid is classified by having exactly one pair of opposite sides that are parallel, while the other pair is not parallel. Four equal sides and four right angles describes a square, which is far more restrictive than a trapezoid. No parallel or equal sides at all describes an irregular quadrilateral, not a trapezoid.

  240. The correct answer is D (12 edges): a rectangular prism has twelve straight edges, formed as four edges along the length, four along the width, and four along the height.   241. The correct answer is C (2 flat faces): a cylinder has two flat, congruent circular faces, one at the top and one at the bottom, connected by a curved surface.   242.   243. The correct answer is D (1 vertex): a cone has exactly one vertex, the single pointed apex located directly above the center of its circular base.   244.   246.   247. The correct answer is D (5 faces): a triangular prism has two triangular end faces plus three rectangular side faces, for a total of five faces.   248. The correct answer is C (Scalene triangle): a scalene triangle is defined as having all three side lengths different from one another, matching the description of no repeating measurements.   249. The correct answer is B (Right angle): a right angle measures exactly 90 degrees and is the angle type that gives a right triangle its name, matching the marked corner in the diagram. An acute angle measures less than 90 degrees, which does not match the small square marker used for the right angle. An obtuse angle measures more than 90 degrees but less than 180 degrees, also not matching this marked corner. A reflex angle measures more than 180 degrees, far larger than any angle inside a triangle.
  250. The correct answer is B (Rhombus and rectangle): a rhombus is a parallelogram defined by four equal sides, while a rectangle is a parallelogram defined by four right angles, so both belong to the parallelogram family without being identical. A trapezoid is not a parallelogram at all, since it has only one pair of parallel sides. A kite is also not a parallelogram, since its sides come in two distinct adjacent pairs rather than opposite parallel pairs. Pairing a trapezoid with either shape breaks the parallelogram-family requirement stated in the question.

Practice Test 3 Answer Key

  1. The correct answer is B (Diameter): a diameter is defined as a straight line segment that passes directly through the center of a circle and connects two points on the circle, equal in length to twice the radius. The radius, shown in the diagram, only connects the center to a single point on the edge, not two points across the circle. The circumference is the total distance around the outside of the circle, not a straight line segment. A chord connects two points on the circle but does not need to pass through the center.
  2. The correct answer is A (A kite has two distinct pairs of consecutive sides that are equal in length): a kite is defined by two pairs of adjacent, or consecutive, sides being equal, rather than opposite sides being equal, which is what makes it distinct from a parallelogram. Option B describes a rhombus, where every side is equal rather than just adjacent pairs. Option C describes a parallelogram, but a kite's sides are generally not parallel at all. Option D describes a rectangle or square, not the typical unequal angle pattern found in a kite.
  3. The correct answer is C (70 degrees): the three interior angles of any triangle always sum to 180 degrees, so subtracting the two given angles, 40 plus 70 equals 110, and 180 minus 110 leaves 70 degrees for the third angle. 50 degrees would only result from an arithmetic slip in the subtraction step. 60 degrees does not correctly balance the equation to reach the required 180 degree total. 110 degrees is the sum of the two given angles, not the value of the missing third angle.
  4. The correct answer is B (Supplementary angles): supplementary angles are defined as two angles whose measures add up to exactly 180 degrees, which matches a straight line split into two adjacent parts. Complementary angles instead sum to 90 degrees, a smaller total than described here. Corresponding angles are formed when a line crosses two parallel lines and refers to matching positions, not a straight-line sum. Alternate angles also relate specifically to parallel lines cut by a transversal, not a simple straight-line pairing.
  5. The correct answer is A (0 edges and 0 vertices): a sphere is a fully curved solid with no flat faces, no straight edges, and no sharp corners, so both its edge count and vertex count are zero. Saying it has 1 edge and 0 vertices incorrectly assigns an edge to a shape with no straight boundary lines at all. Saying it has 0 edges and 1 vertex incorrectly assigns a corner point, but a sphere has no pointed apex anywhere on its surface. Two edges and one vertex describes a cone-like shape, not a sphere.
  6. The correct answer is C (120 degrees): the interior angle sum of a hexagon is 720 degrees, found using (n minus 2) times 180 with n equal to 6, and dividing that total evenly among six equal angles gives 120 degrees each. 60 degrees is the interior angle of an equilateral triangle, not a hexagon. 108 degrees is the interior angle measure of a regular pentagon instead. 135 degrees is the interior angle measure of a regular octagon, a different polygon entirely.
  7. The correct answer is B (The 6 cm side and the 10 cm side): a trapezoid has exactly one pair of parallel sides, and in this diagram those are the two labelled parallel sides measuring 6 cm and 10 cm. There is only one side labelled 6 cm in this trapezoid, so pairing it with itself is not possible. The height is a perpendicular measurement between the parallel sides, not one of the parallel sides itself. All four sides being parallel is impossible in any quadrilateral, since opposite sides at most can be parallel in pairs.
  8. The correct answer is B (6 edges): a triangular pyramid has four triangular faces meeting along six straight edges, three around the triangular base and three connecting the base corners to the apex. Four edges undercounts the shape, since each of its four faces contributes edges shared with neighboring faces. Eight edges overcounts a tetrahedron and instead matches the vertex count of a rectangular prism. Nine edges is the correct count for a triangular prism, a different solid with two triangular bases instead of one.
  9. The correct answer is B (Each corner is a right angle): a small square drawn inside an angle is the standard mathematical symbol used to indicate a right angle measuring exactly 90 degrees, and a rectangle always has four such angles. An acute angle would be marked with a curved arc, not a square symbol, and measures less than 90 degrees. An obtuse angle also uses a curved arc marker and measures more than 90 degrees, not matching a rectangle's corners. A reflex angle is larger than 180 degrees and would never appear inside a simple rectangle.
  10. The correct answer is D (Trapezoid): a general trapezoid has only one pair of parallel sides, so it does not meet the two-pairs-of-parallel-sides requirement that defines a parallelogram. A square always qualifies as a parallelogram because it has two pairs of parallel sides along with equal sides and right angles. A rhombus also always qualifies as a parallelogram, since equal sides on a rhombus still produce two pairs of parallel sides. A rectangle likewise always qualifies as a parallelogram because its four right angles guarantee both pairs of opposite sides are parallel.
  11. The correct answer is A (Obtuse scalene triangle): the 110 degree angle exceeds 90 degrees, making the triangle obtuse, and the three different side lengths of 6 cm, 7 cm, and 11 cm make it scalene at the same time. Acute equilateral is wrong on both counts, since the triangle is not acute and its sides are not all equal. Right isosceles is also wrong, since no angle here measures 90 degrees and only two of the three sides would need to match, which they do not. Obtuse equilateral is impossible in general and also wrong here, since an equilateral triangle can never contain an obtuse angle.
  12. The correct answer is B (Cube): a net made of exactly six congruent squares folds into a cube, since a cube requires six equal square faces meeting at right angles. A rectangular pyramid's net would include one rectangular base and four triangular side faces, not six squares. A triangular prism's net would include two triangles and three rectangles, a completely different shape combination. A cylinder's net includes two circles and one curved rectangle, none of which are squares.
  13. The correct answer is B (2 faces): a cone has one flat circular base and one curved lateral surface, giving a combined total of two faces. One face would ignore either the circular base or the curved surface entirely, undercounting the solid. Three faces overcounts a cone and would only be correct for a cylinder with two flat ends plus a curved side. Four faces greatly overstates the surfaces present on a single-apex curved solid like a cone.
  14. The correct answer is A (The two remote interior angles): the exterior angle theorem states that any exterior angle of a triangle equals the sum of the two interior angles that are not adjacent to it, known as the remote interior angles. The adjacent interior angle alone is actually supplementary to the exterior angle, not equal to it. Summing all three interior angles gives 180 degrees regardless of which exterior angle is chosen, which is not the correct relationship. Taking half of the adjacent angle has no valid geometric basis in this theorem.
  15. The correct answer is A (It has four right angles like a rectangle and four equal sides like a rhombus): a square satisfies the defining property of a rectangle, four right angles, while also satisfying the defining property of a rhombus, four equal side lengths, so it belongs to both categories simultaneously. Option B is false because neither a rectangle nor a rhombus has curved sides, both are made of straight segments. Option C is false because both shapes require parallel opposite sides, not an absence of parallel sides. Option D is nonsensical since both rectangles and rhombuses are four-sided shapes, not three or five-sided ones.
  16. The correct answer is A (Adjacent angles): adjacent angles are defined as two angles that share a common vertex and a common side, or ray, while not overlapping each other, exactly matching the description given. Vertical angles instead form on opposite sides of two intersecting lines and do not share a common ray between them. A reflex angle is a single angle measuring more than 180 degrees, not a description of a relationship between two angles. Complementary angles specifically require the two angle measures to sum to 90 degrees, a numeric condition not mentioned in the stem.
  17. The correct answer is B (60 degrees): an equilateral triangle has three equal sides and three equal angles, and since all interior angles of any triangle sum to 180 degrees, dividing evenly gives exactly 60 degrees for each angle. 45 degrees would only occur in a specific isosceles right triangle, not an equilateral one. 90 degrees describes a single right angle and would make the angle sum exceed 180 degrees if repeated three times. 120 degrees is far too large, since three angles of 120 degrees would sum to 360 degrees, not 180 degrees.
  18. The correct answer is A (Vertices minus edges plus faces does not equal 2): Euler's formula requires vertices minus edges plus faces to equal 2 for any simple polyhedron, but here 8 minus 10 plus 6 equals 4, which fails the check. Saying edges must always exceed vertices is not a rule of Euler's formula and is false for shapes like a cube. Saying faces must always equal vertices is also not a true general rule, since a cube has 6 faces but 8 vertices. Euler's formula applies specifically to polyhedra with flat faces and straight edges, not curved solids, so option D misstates its scope.
  19. The correct answer is C (The side opposite the right angle): the hypotenuse is defined as the longest side of a right triangle, always located directly opposite the 90 degree angle, regardless of the specific leg lengths given. The 9 cm leg is adjacent to the right angle, not opposite it, so it cannot be the hypotenuse. The 12 cm leg is also adjacent to the right angle marker, forming one arm of the right angle rather than facing it. Describing a side as adjacent to the right angle marker actually rules it out as the hypotenuse, since the hypotenuse is never adjacent to the right angle.
  20. The correct answer is B ((n minus 2) times 180, where n is the number of sides): substituting n equals 5 gives (5 minus 2) times 180, which equals 3 times 180, or 540 degrees, matching the pentagon's known angle sum. Using n times 90 with n equals 5 gives only 450 degrees, which does not match the correct total. Using n times 180 with n equals 5 gives 900 degrees, a value far too large for a pentagon's angle sum. Using (n plus 2) times 90 gives 630 degrees, another incorrect result that does not represent any valid polygon angle-sum rule.
  21. The correct answer is A (Cube): when a rectangular prism has equal length, width, and height, all six faces become congruent squares, and this special case is correctly called a cube. A cylinder has curved surfaces and circular ends, which does not match a solid built entirely from flat square faces. A square pyramid has a single square base and triangular sides meeting at an apex, not six square faces. A sphere is a fully curved, round solid with no flat faces or straight edges at all.
  22. The correct answer is B (Corresponding angles): corresponding angles occupy the same relative position at each intersection point when a transversal crosses two lines, and when the lines are parallel these angle pairs are always equal. Vertical angles instead form directly across from each other at a single intersection point, not across two separate intersection points. Complementary angles are defined by a 90 degree sum requirement, which is unrelated to matching positions on a transversal diagram. Reflex angles describe a single angle greater than 180 degrees and are not a pairing term at all.
  23. The correct answer is A (Two identical right triangles): drawing a diagonal across a rectangle splits it into two congruent right triangles, each containing one of the rectangle's original 90 degree corners. Equilateral triangles require all three sides equal, but the triangles formed here have a right angle and clearly unequal leg lengths of 12 cm and 5 cm. Splitting a rectangle with a single straight diagonal produces two triangles, not a trapezoid and a triangle combination. Two parallelograms would require a different cut, such as a line parallel to one of the sides, not a diagonal.
  24. The correct answer is B (The claim is incorrect, since a square also requires four right angles, which a rhombus is not guaranteed to have): a square is a special rhombus that additionally has four right angles, so an ordinary rhombus with non-right angles does not qualify as a square. Option A is false because equal sides alone are not sufficient, since the right angle requirement must also be met. Option C is false because a general rhombus can be slanted, with angles that are not 90 degrees at all. Option D is false because equal sides are precisely the defining property of a rhombus, so it cannot lack them.
  25. The correct answer is C (2 edges): a cylinder has two circular edges, one where the curved surface meets the top circular face and one where it meets the bottom circular face. Zero edges would describe a sphere, which has no meeting lines between distinct surfaces at all. One edge undercounts a cylinder, since both the top and bottom circles each form their own edge. Three edges overcounts the shape, since only two distinct circular boundaries exist on a standard cylinder.
  26. The correct answer is C (Between 180 and 360 degrees): a reflex angle is defined as any angle measuring strictly more than 180 degrees, a straight angle, and strictly less than 360 degrees, a full turn, matching the description given. Between 0 and 90 degrees describes the range for an acute angle, far smaller than a reflex angle. Between 90 and 180 degrees describes the range for an obtuse angle, still smaller than a reflex angle. Exactly 360 degrees represents one complete full turn, a single fixed value rather than the open range that defines reflex angles.
  27. The correct answer is C (9 edges): a triangular prism has three edges around each of its two triangular bases plus three connecting edges running along its length, giving three plus three plus three, which equals nine edges total. Six edges undercounts the shape, since it only accounts for the edges of one triangular base doubled without the connecting edges. Eight edges also undercounts the total number of straight lines that form this solid's boundary. Twelve edges overcounts the shape and instead matches the edge count of a rectangular prism.
  28. The correct answer is B (They are complementary angles): adding 35 degrees and 55 degrees gives exactly 90 degrees, which is the defining sum for a pair of complementary angles. Supplementary angles require a sum of 180 degrees, and this pair falls far short of that total. Vertical angles must be equal in measure to one another, but 35 degrees and 55 degrees are clearly different values. Reflex angles describe a single angle greater than 180 degrees, not a relationship between two smaller angles like these.
  29. The correct answer is C (General quadrilateral): every four-sided closed shape, including a trapezoid, automatically falls under the broad category of general quadrilaterals, since that category only requires four straight sides. A parallelogram requires two pairs of parallel sides, but this trapezoid has only one pair, so it does not qualify. A rhombus requires all four sides to be equal, which is not indicated for this trapezoid with different slanted side lengths. A rectangle requires four right angles and two pairs of parallel sides, neither of which is guaranteed by the trapezoid description given.
  30. The correct answer is C (Sphere): a sphere is entirely curved with no flat faces anywhere on its surface, matching the description of exactly one curved surface and zero flat faces. A cone has one flat circular face in addition to its curved lateral surface, so it does not have zero flat faces. A cylinder has two flat circular faces along with its curved surface, giving it more than the zero flat faces described. A square pyramid is made entirely of flat triangular and square faces with no curved surface at all, the opposite of what the question describes.
  31. The correct answer is A (Because the two equal sides are opposite those two base angles): in any isosceles triangle, the angles opposite the two equal sides are always congruent to each other, which is exactly the relationship shown in the diagram. Not all triangles have two equal angles, since a scalene triangle has three entirely different angle measures. The base angle sum in this triangle is not fixed at 90 degrees, since the total of all three angles must equal 180 degrees instead. Isosceles triangles actually do have a line of symmetry running from the apex to the midpoint of the base, contrary to option D.
  32. The correct answer is C (8 vertices): a rectangular prism has eight corner points, formed where three edges meet at each of the solid's eight corners. Four vertices would only describe a triangular pyramid, a solid with far fewer corners than a rectangular prism. Six vertices matches the face count of a rectangular prism, a number students often mistakenly substitute for the vertex count. Twelve vertices actually corresponds to the edge count of the same solid, not the number of corner points.
  33. The correct answer is C (8 edges): a square pyramid has four edges forming its square base plus four slanted edges running from each base corner up to the apex, giving a total of eight edges. Four edges only accounts for the base and completely ignores the four slanted edges leading to the apex. Six edges undercounts the shape by missing at least two of the required slanted edges.   181. The correct answer is D (Triangulation of evidence, drawing on all three named sources together): Combining watching, questioning, and reviewing written work draws on all three sources of evidence, which is the definition of triangulation. This is not standardized testing, since it is a classroom based, teacher directed process rather than a large-scale external test. It also is not limited to diagnostic assessment or evaluative feedback alone, since it blends multiple sources of evidence together.
  182. The correct answer is C (A written product alone may not fully capture a student's actual reasoning process): A written product alone may miss important information about how a student actually reasoned through a problem, which is why triangulating with observation and conversation is recommended. Written products are not considered the only valid form of evidence, and Growing Success does not prohibit written tests, only relying on them exclusively. Conversations and observations are not automatically less reliable than a product.
  183. The correct answer is C (A teacher asking a student to explain aloud how she arrived at her solution): Asking a student to explain her solution aloud is a direct example of conversation as an evidence source. Grading a worksheet without any student input is instead a product being assessed, not a conversation between teacher and student. A parent signing a report card and a student silently completing a quiz are both unrelated to gathering evidence through conversation.
  184. The correct answer is A (It provides a more complete and accurate picture of learning than any single source): Drawing on three different sources of evidence gives a more complete and accurate picture of a student's learning than relying on any one source by itself. Triangulation does not remove the need to reference curriculum expectations, since professional judgment still relies on them heavily. It also does not guarantee identical grades across students, nor does it eliminate the ongoing need for descriptive feedback.
  185. The correct answer is A (Knowledge of curriculum expectations, achievement levels, and evidence about the student): Professional judgment draws on the teacher's knowledge of curriculum expectations, the achievement chart levels, and the body of evidence gathered about the individual student. Personal opinions about family background have no place in this judgment, since it must remain grounded in actual evidence of learning. A single isolated test score or a comparison of school wide averages does not reflect this process.
  186. The correct answer is B (The use of professional judgment to determine a final grade): Weighing conflicting evidence and giving priority to what is most consistent and recent is precisely how professional judgment is applied when determining a final grade. This scenario is not diagnostic testing, since it occurs after instruction and evidence collection, not before it. It is also not assessment as learning, and it goes beyond simply triangulating feedback, since it involves actively weighing evidence.
  187. The correct answer is B (Recent, consistent evidence often better reflects a student's actual current achievement): A strict average can be skewed by early evidence collected before a student had fully developed a skill, so professional judgment allows teachers to weigh recent and consistent evidence more heavily. This better reflects current achievement than a mechanical average would. Growing Success does not claim averaging always produces the fairest result, and professional judgment still depends on collecting a broad body of evidence.
  188. The correct answer is B (A correct final answer alone does not reveal the reasoning or strategy used): A correct final answer can hide gaps in understanding or a lucky guess, and it does not show the reasoning or strategy a student actually used to get there. Teachers may certainly still assess final answers, just not as the sole source of evidence available to them.
  189. The correct answer is A (Consider the evidence of reasoning and strategy alongside the final answer given): Because the final answer alone does not capture the full picture of understanding, a teacher should weigh the evidence of sound reasoning and strategy along with the outcome. Assigning no credit purely because of a minor calculation slip ignores clear evidence of understanding shown in the work. Ignoring the work and retesting immediately, or averaging with an unrelated assignment, does not use the evidence.
